# 0033 — M22 UI and text contract

- **Status:** accepted. The contract of W22.1, C22.1 ("UI core, text measurement, the UI pass, layout, `ui.tree`, string tables and the input clock"), staged on `milestone/m22` as one contract checkpoint (ADR 0028 decision 11: reviewed, targeted runs in contract mode, never `PreCommit.py` or `CI.py`), then W22.1's integration. The contract of W22.2, C22.2 ("UI input, events, the UI hash block, settings, preview and widgets": decisions 20 to 39), staged on `milestone/m22` as a second contract checkpoint the same way, then W22.2's integration and its review, committed as one checkpoint. The contract of W22.3, C22.3 ("text input, fallback fonts, shaping and bidi, world-space canvases": decisions 40 to 58), staged on `milestone/m22` as a third contract checkpoint the same way, with its review, then W22.3's integration and its review, committed as one checkpoint. The waves' integrations, the merge with main and the milestone's review and gate amend this record with sections of their own: M22 lands after M21, and its landing (the merge of main, the adaptations to M21 and the combined 2D/UI test) is two commits on `milestone/m22` ("Landing"); the milestone review of the whole milestone diff, every finding fixed, is one checkpoint after them ("Milestone review"), and the milestone-end gate, `CI.py --require-clang-cl`, passed ("Gate and landing"). One ADR for the milestone, amended per wave (ADR 0028 decision 15). Lead-owned.
- **Date:** 2026-10-29 (C22.1); 2026-11-02 (C22.2, W22.2's integration and its review); 2026-11-03 (C22.3 and its review); 2026-11-10 (W22.3's integration and its review, the landing, the milestone review and the gate).
- **Base:** `111c1c9` (phase 2's setup on main: the reservation ledger and the skills' reference pages), branch `milestone/m22`; C22.2's base is `3c0cc112` (W22.1 integrated, then main's `4535c203` merged); C22.3's is `5a5da45b` (W22.2 integrated on `fd06249d`, which merged main's `48d68e51`); the milestone review's is `469e663a` (the landing, on main's `969341c2` merged at `0f201a3c`), reviewing `969341c2...469e663a`.
- **Authority:** Roadmap "Rules for every milestone" and M22; ADR 0028 decisions 4, 6, 11, 12, 15, 16 and 17 and its phase 2 reservation ledger; Architecture §1.2, §3, §5.3, §5.7, §6, §7.1, §7.4, §8.2, §8.3, §8.10, §11.5, §13.5, §13.7, §13.8 and §15.6; ADR 0004; ADR 0012 decision 4; ADR 0013 decision 11; ADR 0029 decisions 1, 2, 5 to 10, 14 to 18 and 26 to 33; ADR 0031 decisions 16 and 18. The lead's plan (`bin/Reviews/Backlog-Plan.md`, revision 5: §3.5 "M22", §4.1 to §4.5, §6 "M22", §7, §8 and the appendix rows R-UI-* and W-CLOCK) is kept under the ignored `bin/Reviews/`.

## Context

M22 develops in phase 2 beside M21 (2D) and M23 (3D rendering and assets); M21 and M22 land in readiness order and M23 last, with phase 2's hardened gate (ADR 0028 decisions 12 and 17). M22's milestone end runs `CI.py --require-clang-cl`. Its eighteen items run in three waves (Roadmap M22, plan §3.5):

- **W22.1 (this part, C22.1):** R-UI-CORE (canvas, rectangles, the layout pass), R-UI-TEXT (text measurement, wrapping, character ranges), R-UI-RENDER (the draw list, the Ui pass, UiImage, clipping), R-UI-LAYOUT (stack, grid and flex containers), R-UI-AUTO (`ui.tree`, annotations, skills; the interaction fields filled in W22.2), R-UI-COVER's static half, R-UI-L10N's ranges and string tables, and W-CLOCK (the same-frame input preview).
- **W22.2 (C22.2, decisions 20 to 39):** R-UI-INPUT, R-UI-DET, R-UI-EVENTS, R-UI-WIDGETS, R-UI-SCROLL, R-UI-THEME, R-UI-EDITOR, R-UI-COVER's interactive half; the facts C22.2 relies on are decision 20's.
- **W22.3 (C22.3):** R-UI-TEXTINPUT, R-UI-L10N's fallback fonts and CJK, R-UI-L10N-RTL, R-UI-WORLD.

M22 owns in phase 2 (ADR 0028's ledger): the font and text pipeline (`TextLayout`, `TextRenderer`, `Shared/TextVertex.h`, `FontData`, `FontImporter`) and `Vendor/stb`; the post chain, tonemap, the output stage and `ViewportCapture` for the Ui slot only; `SceneRenderer.cpp`'s SceneOverlay Ui slot; FontData 2 and FontImporter 4 and up; the StringTable format and AssetType name; the FeatureTest IdSeeds 2000 to 2099; the PlaySessionPhases TextLatch, UiInput, UiEvents, UiLayout and FrameUiLayout at the ledger's positions; the ui state-hash block after physics2d; the Ui render pass; the Ui and Localization settings sections; `UiDrawList.h` in `ModuleRules.json`'s SnapshotHeaders; HarfBuzz and SheenBidi (C22.3); M22's fallback fonts at 0x01c2 to 0x01c4 if they are built in (C22.3). It uses M18's QuadBatcher and `QuadVertex.h` as landed (a change takes a ledger amendment), adds its own members to `RenderSnapshot.h` after `Output`, and adds its own paths to `RenderExtraction.cpp` and `RenderAnnotations.cpp` after M23's.

The facts this contract relies on, checked in the code at the base:

1. **`TextComponent` is the only UI.** Screen text is placed by a viewport-normalized anchor at a 1080p reference (`Engine/Source/Engine/Scene/Components/TextComponent.h`), laid out by `Renderer/TextLayout.h`, which ADR 0013 decision 11 froze, and drawn by the TextRenderer, world texts then screen texts.
2. **M18's output stage reserved the UI's place.** UI is laid out and hit-tested in scene pixels (`Renderer/RenderSnapshot.h`'s file comment and `MapViewPointToScenePoint`; ADR 0029 decision 8); the pass list keeps the slot after Text in the SceneOverlay stage (`Renderer/RenderPassDeclaration.h`), which `SceneRenderer::State::RecordSceneOverlayStage` (`Renderer/SceneRendererOutput.cpp`) leaves empty after Text; the QuadBatcher has the Ldr pipeline into the scene overlay's framebuffer, a scissor for M22's clipping and `ExpandNineSlice` (`Renderer/QuadBatcher.h`, ADR 0029 decision 10); a SpriteSheet frame carries its 9-slice borders (`Asset/SpriteSheetData.h`).
3. **Render extraction reads no asset.** `ExtractRenderSnapshot` is a pure function of the scene and the request (`Scene/RenderExtraction.h`), and the renderer resolves meshes, materials and fonts through the AssetManager (`Renderer/TextRenderer.h`: a TextItem names its font by handle and the renderer lays it out). The editor's viewports, `viewport.screenshot` (`Automation/Methods/ScreenshotMethods.cpp`, which no phase-2 milestone may change) and the play session all call it, so whatever it extracts reaches every view without a host change.
4. **Latin-1 is already baked.** FontImporter version 3 bakes ASCII and Latin-1's printable half (`FontCharacterSet`, U+0020 to U+007E and U+00A0 to U+00FF; `AssetPipeline/Importers/FontImporter.cpp`); a font's import settings hold `PixelSize` and `Spread` only.
5. **A frame without a step sees no new input.** Device events are queued for the next tick, and `PlayInput::LatchFrame` latches the frame view of the applied state (ADR 0012 decision 4; Backlog §5's row, scheduled for M22 W22.1).
6. **Scripts, settings and the edit VM.** Script modules register in `Scripting/RegisterBindings.cpp`; `ScriptEngineSpecification` has no locale; the edit evaluation's VM is created in `EditorCore/Automation/EditorMethodContext.cpp` (line 593 at the base), a file the phase-2 ledger gives no owner.
7. **Automation.** The shared scene reads live in `Engine/Source/Engine/Automation/Methods/SceneMethods.*` (`scene.tree` is a tool, in the Runtime subset, batchable and scene-targeted); 99 methods and 65 proxied tools.
8. **The demos hold no UI.** No audited project holds a UI component or a string table; FeatureTest holds two fonts (`Assets/Formats/Fonts`), the demos none.

## Decisions

### 1. What C22.1 freezes, and what it implements

- **Registered by the contract** (ADR 0028 decision 11's shared integration files, which plan §7 item 4 says each contract lands): the six UI components in `BuiltinComponents.h` with their category file `Scene/Registration/UiRegistration.cpp` (decision 2); `ui.tree`'s types and registration in `RegisterSharedMethods.cpp` (decision 8); the `Loc` module in `RegisterBindings.cpp` (decision 11); the Ui pass declaration (decision 7); `AssetType::StringTable` with its names (decision 10); the PlaySessionPhases UiLayout and FrameUiLayout with their names (decision 3); `RenderSnapshot::Ui`; `UiDrawList.h` in `Scripts/ModuleRules.json`'s SnapshotHeaders and Scene's Headers; the `StringTableData` fuzz target's row in `Tests/Data/Fuzz/Targets.json`; `EditorIcon::StringTable` with its glyph and mapping; and the regenerated references (`Docs/Reference/*`, `Resources/Scripting/Engine.d.luau`, `Tools/MCP/catalog.json`).
- **Implemented by the contract** (inert unless used): the component headers and their registration; `UiDrawList.h` and its memory-resource constructor; `UiSystem`'s members and accessors but `GetLayout`; `PlaySession::GetUi`; `ScriptEngineSpecification::Locale` and `ScriptEngine::GetLocale` (no host sets it yet); `GetStringTableDependencies` (final: none); `StringTableImporter`'s id, version, type and extension; the `Loc` registration; `ui.tree`'s types and registration; the Test fixture's `ScriptTestFixtureSpecification::Locale`.
- **C++ stubs:** every new function's first statement is `ENGINE_CONTRACT_STUB();`; a stub returning `Result` or `Status` returns `Unsupported` naming this record, its decision and its stream; any other returns an empty or neutral value (a `Loc` function raises an `Unsupported`-worded script error). 35 stubs: `ComputeUiLayout`, `ComputeUiClipRect`, `FindUiLayoutNode`, `FindUiCanvasLayout` (A); `UiSystem::LayoutStep`, `LayoutFrame`, `GetLayout` (A); `LayoutUiContainer` (D); `MeasureText`, `MeasureTextSize` (B); `PlanUiPass`, `RecordUiPass`, `TextRenderer::RecordGlyphRuns`, `AppendUiDrawItems` (C); `AppendUiRenderAnnotations`, `Automation::UiTree` (E); thirteen functions of `StringTableData.cpp`, `StringTableImporter::Import` and the three `Loc` functions (L); `PlayInput::SetFramePreview` and `IsFramePreviewEnabled` (K). Nothing calls a stub outside its skipped tests, except the registered `ui.tree` handler and `Loc` functions, which answer `Unsupported` until their streams land.
- **Acceptance tests:** final names, marked `doctest::skip(true)` (C++) or `unittest.skip` with a "contract stub" reason (Python): 54 C++ cases (four of them GPU cases) and 4 Python tests, listed under "Acceptance (W22.1)". Each fails when forced against the contract tree: the 50 CPU cases were forced with `--no-skip` and fail (a stub's empty result, or a stub's error); the four GPU cases render through a SceneRenderer whose SceneOverlay stage records no UI yet, so each pixel check, the "Ui" stats row and the loss test's UI pixel fail by construction (GPU cases run only through `Test.py`, which does not force skips).
- **Live tests changed or added** because they pass against the contract tree: "BuiltinComponents: every listed type is plain component data" (30 types), "BuiltinComponents: UI defaults follow the component table" (new), "BuiltinComponents: every exclusion is declared by both components" (new, the review's finding m1), "BuiltinComponents: registry names are those of the component table", "BuiltinComponents: every component has a category, a version and descriptions" (the Ui category); "RenderPassDeclarations: the stats order puts game Text before the upscale and the editor overlays after it" (26 rows, Ui after Text, a frozen contract of `stats.get` that M22 extends) and "... each pass declares the pipelines its class creates" (Ui declares none); "PlaySessionPhase: every phase has its enumerator name"; "AssetType: names round-trip and parsing is case-sensitive" and "AssetType: persisted values never change" (StringTable, 12); "Icons: every glyph draws lines inside its square" and "Icons: every asset type maps to its glyph"; "RegisterMethods: the editor registers exactly the documented method set", "... every method has a description, structs, an example and the documented flags" and the tool count (66); the scene-target inventory ("SceneTarget: every scene-addressing method takes the optional scene target": `ui.tree` is Target); "RuntimeAutomationServer: serves the Runtime subset and only it"; "RegisterBindings: the built-in registry covers the complete scripting surface" (`Loc`); the new "UiDrawList: a list over a memory resource allocates its items and annotations from it", "UiClipRect: a rectangle that holds no pixel is empty" and "RenderExtraction: a scene without a canvas has an empty Ui list"; and `Tools/MCP/tests/test_schemas.py`'s proxied tools (`ui_tree`). The registry suite ("TypeRegistry: ...") covers the six components with no further code. Every existing test is kept.
- **No behaviour changes.** No scene holds a UI component, no host extracts or draws UI yet, `ui.tree` and `Loc` answer `Unsupported`, the frame view latches as before, and the stats list shows a Ui row only when the pass records one. The FeatureTest coverage gates now name the six components, `ui.tree` and `Loc` as uncovered: the feature, automation and export suites are not part of a contract checkpoint, and W22.1's integration covers them (decision 14).

### 2. The six components (R-UI-CORE, R-UI-RENDER, R-UI-TEXT, R-UI-LAYOUT)

A category of their own, **Ui**, after Scripting (ADR 0028's phase-2 ledger: phase 2's categories go after Scripting in landing order; M21's go before or after by landing), registered by `RegisterUiComponents` in `Scene/Registration/UiRegistration.cpp`, each at version 1 with the default flags (serializable, script-visible, editor-visible, removable), in this order:

| Component (registry name) | Fields (defaults, ranges) | Relations |
|---|---|---|
| `UiCanvasComponent` (`UiCanvas`) | `int32 SortOrder{0}`; `UiScaleMode ScaleMode{ConstantPixelSize}` (ConstantPixelSize, MatchHeight); `float ScaleFactor{1}` (0.01 to 100); `float ReferenceHeight{1080}` (1 to 100,000); `bool PixelSnap{true}` | excludes UiRect (declared by both, as RigidBody and CharacterController are) |
| `UiRectComponent` (`UiRect`) | `vec2 AnchorMin{0.5}`, `vec2 AnchorMax{0.5}` (0 to 1; AnchorMin at most AnchorMax per axis, a Validate hook with its Generate hook); `vec2 Pivot{0.5}` (0 to 1); `vec2 Position{0}`, `vec2 Size{100}` (±1,000,000 canvas units); `bool ClipChildren{false}` | excludes UiCanvas |
| `UiImageComponent` (`UiImage`) | `AssetRef<SpriteSheet> Sheet` (null: a plain rectangle of Color); `uint32 Frame{0}` (0 to 65,535); `color4 Color{1}`; `UiImageType Type{Simple}` (Simple, Sliced); `float BorderScale{1}` (0.01 to 1000); `UiImageFilter Filter{Point}` (Point, Linear) | requires UiRect |
| `UiTextComponent` (`UiText`) | `std::string Text`; `AssetRef<Font> Font` (null: the default font); `float Size{32}` (1 to 100,000 canvas units per em); `color4 Color{1}`; `TextAlignment Alignment{Left}` (the Text component's registry enum); `UiTextVerticalAlignment VerticalAlignment{Top}` (Top, Middle, Bottom); `bool Wrap{true}`; `float LineSpacing{1}` (0.1 to 10) | requires UiRect |
| `UiLayoutComponent` (`UiLayout`) | `UiLayoutKind Kind{Stack}` (Stack, Grid, Flex); `UiLayoutDirection Direction{Vertical}` (Horizontal, Vertical); `UiPadding Padding` (registry struct `UiPadding {Left, Top, Right, Bottom}`, each 0 to 1,000,000); `vec2 Spacing{0}` (0 to 1,000,000); `UiMainAlignment MainAlignment{Start}` (Start, Center, End); `UiCrossAlignment CrossAlignment{Start}` (Start, Center, End, Stretch); `uint32 Columns{0}` (0 to 1024); `vec2 CellSize{100}` (0 to 1,000,000); `bool Wrap{false}` | requires UiRect |
| `UiLayoutElementComponent` (`UiLayoutElement`) | `bool IgnoreLayout{false}`; `float Grow{0}`, `float Shrink{1}` (0 to 1000); `vec2 MinSize{0}` (0 to 1,000,000) | requires UiRect |

- **Why these six.** The plan's W22.1 names a canvas and rectangles (R-UI-CORE), images with 9-slice and clipping (R-UI-RENDER), text (R-UI-TEXT) and stack, grid and flex containers (R-UI-LAYOUT): a container component holds the three kinds (one component, so a container switches kind without losing its padding and alignment), and its children's flex weights and opt-out need a per-child component (UiLayoutElement). Clipping is a rectangle's flag (`ClipChildren`), so W22.2's ScrollView clips with it and needs no component of its own.
- **Elements.** A UI element is an entity with a UiRect below a canvas; its parent element is its nearest ancestor with a UiRect below that canvas, or the canvas (an entity without a UiRect in between is transparent to the layout). A UiLayout container places the elements whose parent element it is, in canonical order, so a grouping entity's elements are placed as the container's own (`UiLayoutComponent.h`, `UiRectComponent.h`, `UiLayout.h` and `UiContainerLayout.h` state the same rule). A UiCanvas below another canvas's element starts a canvas of its own, laid out over the whole scene image. A canvas has no rectangle of its own (it excludes UiRect), so a full-screen background is a child with stretched anchors. An entity may hold a UiImage and a UiText together (a button's background and label): the image draws first.
- **Left for later waves, with a stated reason:** a canvas's render mode (C22.3 adds `RenderMode` with World and Screen; a one-value enum now could not be covered by FeatureTest's gate 1, which needs a non-default value of every field); interaction (`RaycastTarget`, widgets, focus) and the canvas's theme font (C22.2). Fields are added while the components are unlanded, so no version changes within M22.
- **The demos' hashes.** No audited project holds a UI component (fact 8), so the components change no hash and need no canonical upgrade.

### 3. The UiLayout and FrameUiLayout phases, and the session's UI (R-UI-CORE; stream A)

- **Positions** (ADR 0028's phase-2 execution order; `PlaySessionPhase` in `Session/PlaySession.h`): **UiLayout** ends the fixed step, after PostStepTransformUpdate, so the layout matches the state the step left; **FrameUiLayout** follows FrameTransformUpdate and precedes AudioUpdate and RenderExtraction, so UI written in `OnUpdate` or `OnLateUpdate` is laid out as the frame draws it. The contract adds both enumerators and their names; stream A runs them (`EnterPhase`), in Play and Simulate, with or without a canvas, and adds them to "PlaySession: step order matches the documented sequence" and "PlaySession: Simulate mode skips the script and audio phases", which change with it (extended, not weakened). C22.2 and C22.3 insert TextLatch and UiInput after ApplyInput and UiEvents after StartFlush, and M21 and M23 their own phases, at the ledger's positions.
- **`UiSystem`** (`Scene/UiSystem.h`), owned by the session (`PlaySession::GetUi`): `LayoutStep` and `LayoutFrame` compute `ComputeUiLayout(scene, sceneSize)` into the step's and the frame's table; `GetLayout` is the later of the two (the frame's when a FrameUiLayout ran after the last UiLayout, the step's when none did), which W22.2's UiInput hit-tests and `ui.tree` reads, so a hit test depends only on the state, never on how many frames ran between two ticks (the ledger).
- **The scene resolution.** Both phases lay out at the session's scene resolution: in W22.1 the session's view size (`GetViewSize`: the game view in live play, the project's window size, `GetDeterministicViewSize`, in every deterministic session), the scene resolution of the default `RenderOutputSettings`. The later lander of M21 and M22 makes it the scene resolution M21's PixelPerfectCamera sets (the combined test, plan §3.5), in the session and in extraction alike: done at M22's landing ("Landing" a to c: the session's `GetUiSceneResolution` and extraction's `Output` both come from `GetCameraOutputSettings` through `ComputeGameViewOutputMapping`).
- **Not simulated state.** Layouts are derived from the scene and the scene resolution; they never reach the state hash. W22.2's ui hash block (after physics2d, empty without a canvas) holds interaction state only.
- **The current scene's layout, always.** Outside the phase list (no observer reports it), `PlaySession::Activate` runs `LayoutStep` of the session's scene both before the scene's startup callbacks (`OnCreate`, `OnStart`) and after them, and so does a `Scene.Load` or `Scene.Reload`'s replacement of the scene at the end of a frame (`ConsumeRequests`: right after `ResetInteraction`, before the new scene's scripts start, and again after its startup): `GetLayout` is empty only before `Activate` and never describes a replaced scene, so the W22.2 Ui module reads the scene's own layout from its startup callbacks (a menu's `OnStart` can `Ui.SetFocus` its first button and `Ui.GetRect` its elements), `ui.tree {target: play}` right after `play.start` lists the UI, and the first UiInput after a load hit-tests the loaded scene (`UiSystem.h`, `PlaySession.h`; "PlaySession: the current UI layout is the scene's from the start and the loaded scene's right after a Scene.Load", and "Ui: a scene's startup callbacks read its own layout and take focus, at the session's start and after a Scene.Reload"). The layout before the startup callbacks was added by the review of W22.2 (its major, the lead's decision 1): until then the module read an empty table at `Activate` and the replaced scene's after a load or reload. *Amended by the milestone review (the lead's decision 5):* the layout after the startup callbacks is made once the world transforms they changed are up to date (`UpdateTransforms` when the scene's revision moved since the last transform update, in `Activate` and in `ConsumeRequests`; the first `InterpolationSnapshot` makes the same update, so interpolation and the hash do not change), so a World canvas an `OnStart` moved is laid out, and hit-tested on the first tick, where it is drawn ("Ui: a World canvas that an OnStart moves takes a click on the first tick where it is drawn").

### 4. Layout (`Scene/UiLayout.h`; R-UI-CORE; stream A)

- **Space.** Scene pixels: x right, y down, origin at the scene image's top-left corner (ADR 0029 decision 8). A canvas covers the scene image; its scale is `ScaleFactor` (ConstantPixelSize) or `sceneHeight / ReferenceHeight` (MatchHeight); its size in canvas units is the scene resolution over its scale.
- **A rectangle in its parent's** (`UiRectComponent.h`): anchor rectangle `A = [P.Min + AnchorMin * P.Size, P.Min + AnchorMax * P.Size]`; size `(A.Max - A.Min) + Size`, each component at least 0; pivot point `A.Min + Pivot * (A.Max - A.Min) + Position`; rectangle `[pivot point - Pivot * size, + size]`; in canvas units, then times the scale from the canvas's origin. A UiLayout parent places its children instead (decision 5), unless a child's UiLayoutElement ignores the layout.
- **PixelSnap** rounds each corner to `floor(v + 0.5)` scene pixels after the scale and before the children are placed in it, so images and clips land on pixel corners.
- **Clip:** the intersection of `ComputeUiClipRect` of every ancestor element with ClipChildren; the element's own flag clips only its descendants. A rectangle covers the pixels whose centres it holds (`min <= x + 0.5 < max`), so `ComputeUiClipRect` is `ceil(v - 0.5)` at both ends (`UiClipRect`, `Renderer/UiDrawList.h`), each clamped to ±2^30 (`UiClipCoordinateLimit`; a NaN to -2^30) before it converts to int32. The registry's ranges do not keep rectangles inside int32 (a MatchHeight canvas scales a unit to up to the scene's height in pixels, and lengths add down a hierarchy), nor inside float's exact integers (2^24): such values are inexact but deterministic, and the clamp keeps the conversion defined.
- **Visible:** the rectangle, intersected with its clip and with the scene image, covers a pixel.
- **The table** (`UiLayoutTable`): the scene resolution; every effectively enabled canvas in draw order (ascending SortOrder, ties in canonical order) with its scale, size and node range; every effectively enabled element of each canvas in canonical order (a parent before its children, siblings in order: painter's order), with its canvas, parent, depth, rectangle, clip and visibility. An effectively disabled element and its subtree are left out and take no space in a container; a UiRect without a canvas ancestor is not laid out (project.validate's `UI_ELEMENT_WITHOUT_CANVAS`, decision 13).
- **Deterministic:** only IEEE-exact float operations (+, -, *, /, comparisons, floor and ceil) on the components' values, in canonical order; no asset access (decision 6). The acceptance "UI layout tables are byte-identical across configurations" is checked by the layout cases' exact expected values in the Debug and Release unit runs, and by the UI replays of W22.2 in the four runs.

### 5. Containers (`Scene/UiContainerLayout.h`; R-UI-LAYOUT; stream D)

`LayoutUiContainer(layout, containerSize, children)` places a container's children (the elements whose parent element it is, decision 2) in canvas units; the header states every rule. In short: the inner rectangle is the container's less Padding; a child's preferred size is its UiRect Size (its UiLayoutElement adds MinSize, Grow and Shrink); **Stack** puts the children one after another along Direction with Spacing, the run placed by MainAlignment (an overflowing run starts at the inner start); **Flex** shares the line's free space by Grow or takes the overflow back by Shrink times size (never below MinSize, without redistribution; a line whose total of Shrink times size is 0 does not shrink and overflows), with Wrap starting new lines, each line as tall as its tallest child; **Grid** gives each child a cell of CellSize, Columns per row (Horizontal) or column (Vertical), or with Columns 0 as many as fit, clamped to 1 to 1024 (1024 when the main axis's cell plus spacing is 0); CrossAlignment places each child across its line (Stretch fills it, a Stack's or Flex's child never below its MinSize; such a child larger than its line follows the same formula, so Center overflows both sides equally and End overflows the start, accepted at W22.1's integration). No rule divides by 0, and every placed size is at least 0. A container's own size never follows its children (decision 6). A pure function, called by stream A's `ComputeUiLayout`; the header is public so the unit tests reach it (a Scene `Private/` header is Scene's alone, Lint's `include-private`).

### 6. Text measurement and character ranges (R-UI-TEXT, R-UI-L10N's ranges; stream B)

- **Beside the frozen `TextLayout.h`** (ADR 0013 decision 11), which keeps laying out TextComponent unchanged: `Renderer/TextMeasurement.h`, `MeasureText(fonts, text, options)` and `MeasureTextSize`, from a font chain's metrics to positioned glyphs in pixels, lines (their byte ranges, left, width, top and baseline) and a block size, with word wrapping (`TextWrap::Word` breaks at spaces, a word wider than `MaxWidth` between glyphs), line feeds, `LineSpacing` and alignment within `MaxWidth` (or the widest line). The header states the contract's rules, and `TextMeasurement.cpp`'s file comment and Architecture §8.10 the finer wrapping rules stream B implemented beside them (W22.1's integration: which space of a run a line breaks at, that a line never wraps empty, where skipped bytes and carriage returns belong, one skipped codepoint per ill-formed byte, the 2^32 - 1 byte bound); the output is identical for equal inputs in every configuration.
- **Shaping and bidi are reserved for W22.3** behind these signatures: `TextDirection` (Auto, LeftToRight, RightToLeft; every value lays out left to right in W22.1), `TextMeasuredGlyph::ByteOffset` (a cluster's first byte once HarfBuzz shapes clusters) and the **font chain** (a codepoint takes the first font of the chain with a glyph for it; W22.1's callers pass one font, W22.3's fallback chain more). C22.3 pins HarfBuzz and SheenBidi and puts them inside `MeasureText`.
- **Scene does not include it.** ADR 0028's ledger adds the measurement header to SnapshotHeaders "if Scene's layout includes it"; it does not: render extraction reads no asset (fact 3), so Scene's layout needs no font, and a rectangle never follows its content (a label is not sized to its text, an image not to its frame, a container not to its children). Text wraps and aligns inside the rectangle it is given, which the renderer lays out with its fonts, as it lays out TextItems (decision 7). The header includes nothing beyond Core, Asset, glm and the standard library, so a later contract may list it if content sizing is ever wanted. Recorded as an accepted limit (Backlog §6).
- **Character ranges:** Latin-1 is already baked (fact 4). Per-font ranges are a new import setting, frozen here and landed by stream B with the registry change: `FontImportSettings.Ranges`, an array of the registry struct `FontCharacterRange {First, Last}` (UInt32 codepoints, `0x20 <= First <= Last <= 0x10FFFF`, at most 64 ranges and 65,536 codepoints in all, located Validation errors otherwise), baked with ASCII and Latin-1 (codepoints the font lacks left out, as today); **FontImporter version 4** (the ledger's "4 and up": every cached atlas is rebuilt once, byte-identical for a font without ranges). `FontData` stays at format 1 in W22.1 (the glyph list grows; C22.3 takes FontData 2 if shaping needs glyph indices). The default font takes no ranges, so its atlas, and the Text golden, do not change.

### 7. The draw list and the Ui pass (R-UI-RENDER; stream C)

- **`Renderer/UiDrawList.h`** (in SnapshotHeaders and Scene's Headers): `UiDrawList {Items, Annotations}` over a memory resource like the snapshot's containers (M18's R-ARENA); a `UiDrawItem` is an image or a text of one element in scene pixels with its entity, rectangle, colour and clip, and for an image its sheet handle, frame, type, border scale (scene pixels per texel) and filter, for a text its string, font handle, size in scene pixels per em, alignments, wrap and line spacing (the draw list's own enums, since the header cannot include Scene); a `UiAnnotation` is a capture's outline or label (decision 9). `RenderSnapshot::Ui` follows `Output` (ADR 0028's ledger).
- **Extraction** (`ExtractRenderSnapshot`, `Scene/UiExtraction.h`'s `AppendUiDrawItems`, stream C's code in `RenderExtraction.cpp` after M23's paths): for the Primary and Entity camera sources (the game view and a camera's view; a view without a primary camera draws its UI, as it draws its screen texts), every canvas laid out at the scene resolution of the snapshot's Output (the view's size by default) and each visible element's image, then its text when not empty, with the canvas's scale applied to the lengths. The editor's scene view (Explicit) draws no Screen canvas; W22.2's authoring overlay decides what the editor shows there. C22.3's World canvases draw in every view that draws the world, the scene view included (decision 51; amended by the review of C22.3, whose minor found this rule and `RenderExtraction.h`'s still saying that the scene view draws no game UI). Extraction lays out afresh: the layout is a pure function, so the game view's draw list equals the session's frame layout at the same scene resolution, and a screenshot at another size lays out at its own (fact 3: every host gets UI without a change).
- **The Ui pass** (`Renderer/UiPass.h`): `PlanUiPass` resolves each item's sheet (no placeholder: a missing or failed sheet, or a frame it lacks, skips the item and counts it; a missing sheet, an asset of another type or a failed load is also reported once per plan through `AssetManager::ReportDiagnostic`, as `GetOrPlaceholder` reports a missing mesh or font, with `ASSET_MISSING`, `ASSET_TYPE_MISMATCH` or `ASSET_IMPORT_FAILED` saying that the image draws nothing, logged once per manager: W22.1's review), expands a Sliced frame with `ExpandNineSlice`, lays out each text with `MeasureText` in its rectangle (wrap at its width, the vertical alignment's offset), and splits the result into runs in draw order where the kind, the clip, or a glyph run's font or colour changes; `RecordUiPass` records each quad run with **M18's QuadBatcher as landed** (the Ldr pipeline, `MakeQuadPixelTransform` of the framebuffer, the run's clip as `QuadBatchInputs::Scissor`, textures through `GpuResourceCache::GetTexture`, the sampler from the filter) and each glyph run with the TextRenderer's new **`RecordGlyphRuns`** (M22 owns the text pipeline: pre-laid-out glyphs in pixels, one font and colour per run, an optional scissor, the screen vertices on top). The SceneOverlay stage records the "Ui" row after "Text" (`SceneRendererOutput.cpp`), into the scene overlay's framebuffer at the scene resolution, before the Output stage's upscale and under the editor overlays; the data views draw no UI, as they draw no Text.
- **The pass declaration:** "Ui" in the SceneOverlay stage after Text, no pipeline of its own (it records with the QuadBatcher's and the Text pipelines), so the startup count stays 58 and the stats order has 26 rows: ..., Text, Ui, Upscale, ... (ADR 0028's phase-2 stats order, where M21's and M23's rows join later).
- **The recreation protocol** (C18.3; plan §7 item 3): the pass creates no GPU object of its own (its batcher and text renderer are SceneRendererPipelines', textures the GpuResourceCache's, font atlases the TextRenderer's, binding sets from the view's PassBindingCaches, which stream C adds to SceneRenderer's state), so the placement rule covers it through those owners; its loss test, "UiPass: a device loss recreates nothing of the pass's own and the UI draws the same image afterwards", runs through `Test::RunDeviceLossTest`.
- **Acceptance:** "A 1:1 UI image reads back pixel-exact" ("UiPass: a 1:1 UI image reads back its source texels pixel-exact", under both API caps) and "the scissor oracle holds" ("UiPass: a clipped element leaves the pixels outside its clip untouched").
- **Textures for M23's `Compression: None`** (ADR 0028's ledger: each sibling's ADR lists the textures its scaffolds, importers and creation paths make): W22.1 adds no importer or creation path that makes a texture. The textures M22's FeatureTest scaffolds import (decision 14), listed by W22.1's integration: `Assets/Textures/GoldenUi/Rgba.png` (`UiGolden.jsonl`, imported with `GenerateMips` off, the texture of its `Ui.spritesheet`). `Ui.jsonl` imports none: its images use Formats' `Tiles.spritesheet`, whose texture M23 covers as a sprite sheet's texture.

### 8. `ui.tree` (R-UI-AUTO; stream E)

- **`ui.tree {canvas?, depth?, format?, width?, height?, target?}`** (`Engine/Automation/Methods/UiMethods.h`), shared by the editor and the Runtime: the target scene's UI laid out in scene pixels. The play scene reads the session's current layout (`UiSystem::GetLayout`) at its scene resolution unless `width` and `height` ask for another; the edit scene is laid out at `width` x `height`, by default the project's window size (`GetDeterministicViewSize`, the view of every deterministic session). `canvas` (an EntityRef) narrows it to one canvas; `depth` limits the levels below each canvas (0: unlimited); `format` is `SceneTreeFormat`'s Text or Json.
- **The result:** `{scene, width, height, text | elements}`; an element (`UiTreeEntry`) is `{id, name, path, canvas, parent, depth, components, rect, sortOrder, scale, clipped, clip, visible, text}` (`sortOrder` and `scale`, the scene pixels per canvas unit at the resolution laid out at, are a canvas's, 0 for an element, as the text outline prints them) (rectangles as `UiPixelRect {x, y, width, height}`; the text cut to 256 bytes at a UTF-8 boundary) plus the **interaction fields reserved for W22.2**, reported as their defaults until then: `interactable`, `focused`, `hovered`, `pressed` and `widget` (the widget kind W22.2 names; W22.3's `TextInput`). The text outline: a header line `<file name>  rev <n>  <width>x<height> scene pixels  <count> elements`, then per canvas `<name> <8 hex> UiCanvas(sort <n>, scale <s>)` and per element a box-drawn line with its name, 8 hex digits, short component forms (`UiImage(<sheet path>#<frame>)`, `UiText("<text>")`, `UiLayout(<kind> <direction>)`), its rectangle `(x,y wxh)` and `clipped` or `hidden`.
- **Flags:** a tool (`ui_tree`; §13.8: 66 proxied tools), `AvailableInRuntime`, `AllowedInBatch`, `SupportsSceneTarget` (the scene-target inventory's Target: it reads the addressed scene, ADR 0031 decision 16), not mutating, no dry run (a read). Errors: the target's and `canvas`'s (ResolveEntity at `/canvas`); InvalidArgument at `/canvas` for an entity without a UiCanvas, and for one of `width` and `height` alone.
- **Parity:** the UI the editor shows (its game view, decision 7) is read by `ui.tree` and captured by `viewport.screenshot`; the method has its C++ round trip ("UiMethods: ...") and its Python tests (`Tests/Automation/test_ui.py`, the Runtime's included), and gate 5.

### 9. Annotations and skills (R-UI-AUTO; stream E)

- **Capture annotations** (`viewport.screenshot`'s `annotate`, §8.13): `AppendRenderAnnotations` also calls `AppendUiRenderAnnotations` (`Scene/UiExtraction.h`), which, from the snapshot's own UI draw list, adds an Outline in yellow `(1, 1, 0, 1)` around every element with a draw item when `bounds` is asked, and a Label in white (name and the first six hex digits of its id, as the entity labels read) for the elements the label mode selects (All, Selected, Explicit). The Ui pass draws them after the items, unclipped, at the scene resolution: outlines as four one-pixel quads, labels in the Default font at 13 scene pixels per em (`UiAnnotationLabelSize`).
- **Skills:** a `skills/game-building/ui` reference page (canvases, rectangles and anchors, containers, images on sprite sheets, text, string tables and `Loc.Get`, `ui.tree`, the frame preview's effect on `OnUpdate` input), the index line in game-building's `SKILL.md`, the two validation codes' rows in `skills/game-debugging/validation-codes`, and a UI section of luau-gameplay where scripts set UI text from string tables; every `SKILL.md` and page stays under `docs.get`'s inline limit (ADR 0028's ledger).

### 10. String tables (R-UI-L10N; stream L)

- **The type:** `AssetType::StringTable`, 12 on M22's branch (ADR 0028's phase-2 ledger: the first of M21 and M22 to land takes 12, the other 13, renumbering its own at its merge and rebuilding its checkouts' cooked caches), spelled `StringTable` in `.meta` files, the script API (`Field.Asset("StringTable")`) and automation. No placeholder table.
- **The file:** `.stringtable`, format `StringTable` version 1, canonical JSON written in full (§6), an authored extension (load and save byte-identical; `ModuleRules.json`'s `Json` list). The data (`Asset/StringTableData.h`): the registry structs `StringTable` (`StringTableData`: `Locales`, `Entries`) and `StringTableEntry` (`Key`, `Values` a Map from locale to string); the header's conventions are the rules: locales are 1 to 35-byte tags of ASCII letters, digits and '-' starting with a letter, unique ignoring ASCII case, the first the fallback; keys 1 to 256 bytes of UTF-8 without control characters, unique; values at most 16,384 bytes of UTF-8 (line feeds and tabs allowed); every entry has the fallback's value; at most 64 locales and 65,536 entries; `ValidateStringTable` reports one located issue per violation in document order.
- **Lookups** (`ResolveStringTableLocale`, `FindStringTableEntry`, `FindLocalizedString`): the table's locale equal to the requested one ignoring ASCII case, else the one equal to its language (before the first '-'), else the fallback; the entry's value for it, or the fallback's. Byte-wise, no locale database, deterministic.
- **The cooked format:** a binary payload, FormatVersion 1, little-endian (the header documents it byte for byte): the locales, then the entries **sorted by key** (so a loaded table binary-searches), each with a presence byte and string per locale. The reader checks every count against its limit and the bytes left, refuses a presence byte other than 0 or 1, entries out of order and trailing bytes, then validates. Its fuzz target, `StringTableData` (`Tests/Data/Fuzz/Targets.json`, added by the contract), mutates the document and the cooked form: the plan placed it in W22.3's stream E, but the reader lands in W22.1, and ADR 0028 decision 12 has a wave that adds a reader of external input run its fuzz case in its targeted unit run, so it moves to stream L (accepted by the lead, 2026-10-29).
- **The importer** (`AssetPipeline/Importers/StringTableImporter.h`): id `StringTable`, version 1, `.stringtable`, no settings, strict (an unknown member fails the import), one artifact, `Import.Dependencies` set explicitly to `GetStringTableDependencies` (none: a table references no asset; ADR 0028's ledger). Registered with its source format (`Asset/SourceFormats.cpp`, gate 9) and its loader (`RegisterBuiltinLoaders`); `ReadCookedArtifact` accepts the type. No validation code (a bad table fails its import).
- **Authoring:** `asset.create {type: StringTable, path, values?}` (`AssetCreateType::StringTable`, appended), `asset.getProperties` and `asset.setProperties` as for a sprite sheet (refusals located under `/values`); the content browser's Create popup gains "String table" after "Sprite sheet" (ADR 0028's ledger: the create list in landing order), which submits `asset.create` without values (a table with the locale "en" and no entries); tables show `EditorIcon::StringTable` (implemented by the contract). The reference generator documents the format (`FileFormats.md`, `Schemas/StringTable.schema.json`).
- **Export:** the exporter cooks every registered asset; M19's pruning follows `Import.Dependencies` and the literal `Assets.Load` scan, so a table a script loads by literal path is kept.

### 11. `Loc` and the locale setting (R-UI-L10N; stream L)

- **The `Loc` module** (registered by the contract): `Loc.Get(table: AssetRef, key: string) -> string` (the key's string in the VM's locale; a key the table lacks, an asset that is not a string table or a table that fails to load raises a located error naming them, with the closest keys as a hint), `Loc.Has(table, key) -> boolean` (an asset that is not a string table or a table that fails to load raises, as in `Get`), `Loc.GetLocale() -> string`; all non-mutating, Runtime and Test environments. A table loads through `LoadAssetAsHost`, so an uncached import counts no callback time.
- **The locale:** `ScriptEngineSpecification::Locale`, fixed for the VM's life and read by `ScriptEngine::GetLocale`; empty reads every table's fallback. The play session passes the project's **`Localization.Locale`**, a new settings section after Testing (ADR 0028's ledger: M22's Ui and Localization sections in landing order; the Ui section is C22.2's): `LocalizationSettings {std::string Locale{}}`, empty or a tag of decision 10's form (a located Validation error otherwise), frozen here and landed by stream L with its registry change, `Tests/Data/Project/AllSettings.eproj`'s value (observed by "ProjectSettings: every AllSettings.eproj value is observed by its consumer", gate 8) and the regenerated canonical templates and fixtures. A `Scene.Load` creates the next VM with the same locale. The edit evaluation's VM reads the project's locale too, one designated initializer in `EditorMethodContext.cpp`'s VM creation, a file the phase-2 ledger gives no owner: the ledger amendment decision 17 requested is granted (amended on main 2026-10-29), and stream L adds it.
- **No runtime switch:** a script cannot change the locale (an accepted limit, Backlog §6): a game's language menu takes effect at the next session or export. The determinism of a replay follows: the locale is project data, the same in the four runs.
- **The demos:** the new section changes every `.eproj`'s canonical form, never a hash; the lead's integration redoes the recorded `Editor --headless --project <P> --upgrade` of each audited project on the merged tree (ADR 0028's ledger), and `Scaffold.py` regenerates FeatureTest.

### 12. W-CLOCK: the same-frame input preview (stream K)

- **Acceptance** (Roadmap M22): "A frame that runs no step sees new input while the step view is unchanged."
- **`PlayInput`** (`Session/PlayInput.h`): `SetFramePreview(bool)` and `IsFramePreviewEnabled()` (off at creation). With the preview on, `LatchFrame` latches the frame view from the devices' state with the **device events** queued for `GetNextTick()` (`QueueDeviceEvent`'s, in queue order, as `ApplyTick` will apply them) applied on a preview copy; the step view, the applied state, the queue and the next `ApplyTick` are unchanged, and a previewed edge is reported once by the frame view (the frame after the tick that applies it sees no second press). Injected events (`Queue`: automation, replays, `Test.Inject*`) are never previewed: they belong to the tick that applies them.
- **The session's policy** (`PlaySession`'s LatchFrame phase): the preview is on only while the session is neither in lockstep nor recording, so lockstep, recording, replaying and test sessions latch exactly as before and every replay keeps its hash (a recording replays with the frame views its ticks gave). The policy, not the hosts, keeps the preview out of deterministic runs: a lockstep or recording session can still receive device input (the Runtime's window queues it in lockstep, the editor's Game view while recording), and the policy switches the preview off for both; replay and test sessions receive no device input (their hosts queue none while a replay or FeatureTest owns input). So the preview never touches a deterministic run. (Corrected by W22.1's review: this bullet first said that deterministic sessions receive no device input at all.)
- **The ledger:** LatchFrame changes in place (no PlaySessionPhase). Stream K amends Architecture §4.3 and §5.7's frame step 1, and W22.1's integration closes Backlog §5's row.

### 13. Validation codes (stream E)

Appended after `PREFAB_CYCLE` in both of `ProjectValidator.cpp`'s tables, kept in step, each with its `Tests/Data/Validation/<CODE>/` fixture (`MakeValidationFixtures.py`) and its row in `skills/game-debugging/validation-codes` (ADR 0028's ledger):

| Code | Severity | Description and fix |
|---|---|---|
| `UI_ELEMENT_WITHOUT_CANVAS` | warning | "A UI element (an entity with UiRect) has no UiCanvas on an ancestor, so it is never laid out or drawn." Fix: move it below a canvas, or add a UiCanvas to its root. No auto-fix. |
| `UI_IMAGE_FRAME_OUT_OF_RANGE` | warning | "A UiImage's Frame is not an index of its sprite sheet's Frames, so it draws nothing." Fix: set Frame below the sheet's frame count, or add the frame. No auto-fix. |
