## Milestone review

One review pass of the whole milestone diff (`969341c2...469e663a`: W22.1 to W22.3, the merge of main and the landing; ADR 0028 decision 11), read-only, in three lenses (correctness and robustness; determinism, formats and integration; tests, parity and documents), each reading every new first-party file in full and the context of every hunk, and not re-raising what the waves' reviews fixed: 26 findings, two of them reported by two lenses (the World canvases' interpolation alpha, by correctness as a major and by determinism as a minor; the letterbox press, by correctness and tests), so 24: one major, 11 minor and 12 nits. Every one was checked against the code and confirmed; every one is fixed in this checkpoint, and none is rejected (the tile brush finding's scenario is corrected below: the missing test was real, the failure it described needs both of the preview's guards removed). The lenses found sound, among others: one scene resolution for the session, extraction, `ui.tree`, the preview and the anchor presets (`GetCameraOutputSettings`, `ComputeGameViewOutputMapping`), the PixelPerfectCamera ray, the ledger's merge positions (phase order, hash blocks, `LdrDepth` at index 4 with 62 startup pipelines, `StringTable` 13, settings sections, validation codes, categories, registries), the combined test's geometry, the text pipeline's readers and lifetimes, the vendored records, the simulation path (IEEE-exact operations, canonical scans, no configuration-dependent code) and the `Suites.jsonl` union itself (83 suites, none lost).

**The lead's decisions on the review** (binding):

1. World canvases at the scene's interpolation alpha (the major): pass the extraction request's alpha to the World canvases' placement, as meshes, sprites, texts and the camera take it, with a regression test at an alpha below 1 (and alpha 1 unchanged); the exported game's own view, the goldens and the replays must not move.
2. The letterbox press: the UI hit test rejects a pointer outside the scene image, as `viewport.pick` ignores letterbox pixels, for Screen and World canvases alike, with regression tests under a PixelPerfectCamera with letterbox bars; the landing record's claim corrected to what the test proves.
3. Capture annotations of World-canvas elements an orthographic or PixelPerfect camera clips away: clipped with decision 50's near and far rule (view-space depth, not clip-space w), with a regression test with an orthographic camera.
4. `Ui.SetFocus` from outside the phases lost at the next UiInput: the focus check and the stored layout agree, choosing the way that keeps every replay's hash, with a regression test that sets focus outside the phases and steps a tick.
5. The layout after the startup callbacks: the transform update they need runs before it, with a regression test (an `OnStart` that moves a World canvas, then a press on the first tick).
6. `Time.SetTimeScale(0)` stops the UI: an accepted limit, not a code change, recorded in Backlog §6, decision 24's amendment, Architecture's UI section and the skills' UI pages (the pause pattern), with a test that pins it.
7. A text-input editing key bound to Cancel, Next or Previous: it does not fire the command while a text input has focus (other keys and gamepad buttons still do; Submit keeps its meaning), with a regression test with Backspace bound to Cancel; a FeatureTest replay whose hash changes is explained, re-recorded on its recording path and verified in the four runs, the demos keeping theirs.
8. `Suites.jsonl`'s dropped trailing `project.refreshAssets`: restored; `Scaffold.py` and `--check`.
9. `FontImportSettings.Fallbacks` without FeatureTest coverage: a FeatureTest UI font with a Noto fallback draws a mixed Latin and Hebrew label in Editor, Release and Dist, checked by a FeatureTest suite's case, where no committed replay's hash changes, with seeds after the combined test's.
10. The PixelPerfectCamera editor adaptations without tests: a UI preview drag test at scale 3 with letterbox bars, a `UiRect` anchor-preset test under a PixelPerfectCamera, the tile brush and UI preview test, and a session-level test of a World canvas through a PixelPerfectCamera; the landing's "no case of its own" texts amended.
11. Every documentation finding and every nit fixed as proposed.
12. Main is not merged in this step.

**Major**

- **M1. World canvases were placed at the scene's interpolation alpha, so `PlaySession::ExtractView` in live play drew them up to a tick ahead of the meshes and the camera** (`Scene/UiExtraction.cpp`; correctness, and determinism as a minor). Confirmed: `GetUiWorldCanvasRenderMatrix` read `scene.GetInterpolationAlpha()`, which the session sets to the frame's only inside its RenderExtraction phase and resets to 1 after it, while `ExtractView` extracts at `GetViewAlpha()`, the last frame's alpha in live play: the editor's Game and Scene views during play, `viewport.screenshot` and `viewport.pick` of the play scene and the Runtime's test screenshots drew a World canvas at the newest tick's pose over meshes at the interpolated one. Fixed (decision 1): `AppendUiDrawItems` takes the extraction's alpha (`float alpha = 1.0f`, appended to M22's frozen signature) and `ExtractRenderSnapshot` passes `request.Alpha`, so a World canvas stands at `ComputeRenderedWorldMatrix(entity, request.Alpha)` times its placement, as a mesh on its entity; `UiExtraction.h`, `RenderExtraction.h`, decision 51's amendment and the integration's stream-choice line say so. The session's own game view (extracted at the scene's alpha, the same value), every lockstep, paused or tick-driven capture, the goldens and the replays are unchanged, as the runs below show. "RenderExtraction: a World canvas is placed at the request's interpolation alpha, as the meshes are" (a runtime scene's canvas moving from x 0 to 4, extracted at alpha 0.25 with the scene's alpha 1, stands at x 1, its entity's rendered pose; at alpha 1 at x 4) failed without the fix (at x 4).

**Minor**

- **m1. A pointer in a PixelPerfectCamera's letterbox hit UI whose rectangle reached past the scene image** (`Scene/UiSystem.cpp`, `EditorCore/Ui/UiAuthoring.cpp`; correctness and tests). Confirmed: the pointer's mapping does not clamp, and the hit test checked the element's rectangle and clip only, so a Screen element bleeding past the image's edge, and a World canvas the ray met beyond the view's edge, took a press from a bar pixel that shows neither; the preview's pick alike. Fixed (decision 2): UiInput's step 2 hit-tests only a pointer inside the scene image ([0, SceneSize) of the layout), so outside it nothing is hovered, pressed or scrolled and no World canvas is reached along the ray (`HitTestUi` and `HitTestUiPointer` stay pure geometry; a drag under way still follows the pointer into a bar, so a slider dragged past its end clamps), and `PickUiElement` picks nothing outside the image; `UiInteraction.h`'s file comment, `UiSystem.h`'s step 2, `UiAuthoring.h`, decisions 24 and 50's amendments, Architecture §4.3 and §5.7 and the game-building `ui` page say so, and "Landing" b's claim now says what its case proved and what the milestone review made hold. "PlaySession: under a PixelPerfectCamera a click in the letterbox reaches no UI, though an element or a World canvas reaches past the image" (a 200 x 120 camera in a 640 x 360 view: clicks in the left bar over "Bleed", whose rectangle starts at scene x -10, and over a World canvas reaching past the view's left edge hover, press and click nothing, while clicks inside the image click both) and "UiAuthoring: the UI preview picks nothing outside the scene image, where an element reaches past its edge" failed without the fix (the bar's clicks hovered and pressed both; the preview picked the overhanging elements).
- **m2. Capture annotations outlined World-canvas elements that an orthographic camera clips away** (`Scene/UiExtraction.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed: the only rejection was a corner's clip-space w, which an orthographic projection (a PixelPerfectCamera's among them) keeps at 1, so a canvas behind such a camera, before its near plane or beyond its far plane, was annotated over a scene that shows nothing there, as was a perspective canvas between the camera and its near plane. Fixed (decision 3): a corner whose view-space depth is below `NearClip`, or above `FarClip` for an orthographic camera, leaves the element unannotated (decision 50's clip range; a perspective camera's far plane is at infinity); `UiExtraction.h` and decision 51's amendment say so. "RenderAnnotations: a World canvas's element is annotated only within the camera's clip range" (an orthographic camera's canvases between its planes annotated, behind it, before its near plane and beyond its far plane not; a perspective canvas before its near plane not, a far one annotated) failed without the fix (4 of its 8 checks).
- **m3. A focus `Ui.SetFocus` took outside the phases was undone at the next UiInput by the stale layout** (`Scene/UiSystem.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed: `SetFocus` checks against the scene as it is, while UiInput's step 1 cleared a focus the stored layout did not hold, and outside the phases (`script.eval {context: Play}` between ticks) nothing lays the scene out again, so a menu shown and focused there lost its focus at the next tick with a Focus (false). Fixed (decision 4), the way that keeps every hash: step 1 checks a focus the stored layout does not hold against a layout of the scene as it is (`LayOutUiNow`, SetFocus's own) and keeps it when focusable there; inside the phases a layout follows every script's change, so both agree and nothing changes (`SetFocus` storing its layout was not taken: it would move what UiInput hit-tests and `ui.tree` reads after an external edit). `UiSystem.h` and decision 28's amendment say so. "PlaySession: a focus a script sets between two ticks on a menu it has just shown is kept at the next tick" (a script evaluation between ticks shows a disabled menu and focuses its button; the next ticks keep the focus and deliver only Focus (true)) failed without the fix.
- **m4. The layout after the startup callbacks kept a World canvas at its pose from before them** (`Session/PlaySession.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed: no transform update ran between `StartScripts` and the post-startup `LayoutStep`, in `Activate` or after a `Scene.Load` or `Scene.Reload`, so a World canvas an `OnStart` moved kept its old matrix in the layout the first tick hit-tests, while extraction drew it at its new pose. Fixed (decision 5): both run `UpdateTransforms` before that layout when the scene's revision moved since the last transform update (the first `InterpolationSnapshot` makes the same update, so interpolation, the hash and every replay are unchanged); `UiSystem.h`, `PlaySession.h` and decision 3's amendment say so. "Ui: a World canvas that an OnStart moves takes a click on the first tick where it is drawn" (at the session's start and after a `Scene.Reload`) failed without the fix (the press missed both times).
- **m5. A time scale of 0 stops the whole UI, and no document said so** (`Session/PlaySession.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed: UiInput, the text latch and UiEvents run only in the fixed step, which a time scale of 0 never runs. Kept as an accepted limit (decision 6), not a code change, since UI input runs in the fixed step for determinism: Backlog §6's row, decision 24's amendment, Architecture §4.3 and §5.7, the game-building `ui` page's Pausing bullet and luau-gameplay's UI section give the pattern (a pause menu keeps the time scale and pauses gameplay with a flag its behaviours check). "PlaySession: with a time scale of 0 live play runs no tick, so a click fires nothing until the scale returns" pins it (frames driven as a live host drives them, through a `FixedStepScheduler` at the session's time scale: 30 frames at scale 0 run no tick and raise no event; at scale 1 the click is processed); it passes with and without the review's changes, as a test of documented behaviour does.
- **m6. A text-input editing key bound to Cancel, Next or Previous both edited the field and fired the command** (`Scene/UiSystem.cpp`, `Session/PlaySession.cpp`; determinism). Confirmed: step 7a kept every Next, Previous and Cancel while a text input had focus, whatever key pressed it, and FeatureTest's and `AllSettings.eproj`'s `Ui.Cancel` hold `Key.Backspace`. Fixed (decision 7): the session fills `UiInputFrame::NonEditingNavigation` (appended to M22's frozen frame) with the commands a gamepad button or a key other than Backspace, Delete, Left, Right, Home and End pressed, and step 7a keeps only its Next, Previous and Cancel, so Backspace bound to Cancel only deletes and Home bound to Next only moves the caret, while Escape, Tab and the gamepad still navigate; Submit and Enter keep their meaning. `UiInteraction.h`, `UiSystem.h`, decision 47's amendment, Architecture §5.7 and the game-building `ui` page's Typing bullet say so. "PlaySession: a key a focused text input edits with fires no Cancel, Next or Previous bound to it" (Backspace bound to Cancel and Home to Next: Backspace deletes and Home moves the caret with the focus kept and no Cancel; Escape then cancels and Tab moves the focus) failed without the fix (Home moved the focus away, and nine events instead of six). `TextInput.replay`, whose recording suite taps Backspace in a field while FeatureTest binds it to Cancel, raised an `OnUiCancel` nothing handles, and keeps its hash, as every replay does (the runs below).
- **m7. The merge dropped `Suites.jsonl`'s trailing `project.refreshAssets`** (`Projects/FeatureTest/Scaffold/Suites.jsonl`; determinism). Confirmed: both parents had the line since M14 (`16f1c806`), and the merge's hand-made union of the patch line left a one-line file, against `Scaffold.py`'s rule that each scaffold ends with a refresh. Fixed (decision 8): the line is restored after the patch line; `Scaffold.py --check` reproduces FeatureTest byte for byte with it.
- **m8. `FontImportSettings.Fallbacks` had no FeatureTest coverage in any run mode** (`Projects/FeatureTest/Scaffold`; tests). Confirmed: only the golden scaffold's copy of Inter names fallbacks, and golden scenes render in `Tests.exe` alone. Fixed (decision 9): `UiFallback.jsonl` (IdSeed 2092) imports Noto Sans Hebrew with the Hebrew block's `Ranges` and a copy of Inter whose `Fallbacks` names it into `Assets/Ui/FallbackFonts/`, and writes `UiFallback.scene`, a label "Shalom" with Hebrew letters in that copy, and the suite `UiFallback`, whose case reads the label's font and text and renders the scene through `Test.Screenshot` in Editor, Release and Dist; no script loads the Hebrew font, so an export keeps it through Inter's import dependencies alone. A scene and suite of their own, so no replay moves (decision 55's amendment, the landing's IdSeed table, Architecture §15.5, `Projects/FeatureTest/AGENTS.md` and `Tests/Data/LICENSES.md`'s font directories).
- **m9. The game-building `ui` page still defined scene pixels as the view's size** (`.claude/skills/game-building/ui.md`; tests). Confirmed. Fixed: the scene image is the view's size or, under a primary camera's PixelPerfectCamera, its reference resolution, upscaled into the view between letterbox bars (a view point p is scene pixel (p - offset) / scale, and a bar reaches no UI); `MatchHeight` scales with the scene image's height, and `ConstantPixelSize` with `ScaleFactor` 1 suits pixel art.
- **m10. Two of the landing's PixelPerfectCamera editor adaptations had no regression test, and the preview's case could not see the letterbox offset** (`Editor/Ui/UiAuthoringOverlay.cpp`, `Editor/Drawers/UiRectDrawer.cpp`; tests). Confirmed. Fixed (decision 10): "SceneViewportPanel: the UI preview picks and drags through a PixelPerfectCamera's letterboxed upscale, and the letterbox picks nothing" (a 200 x 120 camera in a 640 x 360 game frame, scale 3 between 20-pixel bars: a click left of the panel by the bar's offset picks nothing and one inside picks it, a 30-frame-pixel drag of its right edge grows it by 10 units, and a click in the bar clears the selection though "Bleed" reaches into it) failed on a build without the drag delta's `outputScale` and `toPanel`'s `outputOffset` (no drag, the size kept) and without m1's fix (the bar's click selected Bleed); "UiRectDrawer: under a PixelPerfectCamera a preset keeps the rectangle in the reference resolution's canvas" (a 320 x 180 camera in a 1280 x 720 frame: bottom-right keeps the rectangle at (-310, -160)) failed on a build whose drawer laid out at the frame's size ((-1270, -700)); and "PlaySession: under a PixelPerfectCamera a click on a World canvas's button through the camera clicks it" (a 2 x 2-unit canvas 50 units ahead, which the Camera's own perspective ray would miss by 27 units, clicked through the PixelPerfectCamera's orthographic ray) covers the session's ray. "Landing" e's "no case of its own" is amended.
- **m11. The merge's tile brush and UI preview interplay had no test** (`Editor/Panels/SceneViewportPanel.cpp`; tests). Confirmed: neither the brush's cases nor the preview's ran the combination. Fixed (decision 10): "SceneViewportPanel: an armed tile brush paints nothing when a click lands in the UI preview" (an armed brush on a selected tilemap, the preview on, a click over a full-frame UI element: the element is selected, the tilemap file and the history unchanged, no pick requested). The finding's scenario is corrected: removing `|| previewFrame.has_value()` from the brush's `Captured` flag alone paints nothing, since the overlay's item owns the preview's presses and the image item that sets `leftPressed` is not drawn under it; the case failed only on a build with both guards removed (a tile painted, one more undo step, the element not selected). "Landing" f's "no case of their own" is amended to name the case and the two guards.

**Nits**

- **n1. A "Ui" stats row with no draws** (`Renderer/SceneRendererOutput.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed: a view without a camera draws no World canvas, yet a plan of World canvases' runs alone opened the row. Fixed as proposed: the stage records the row only when the plan holds a run the pass draws (a Screen canvas's, or any with a camera), and its comment says so. "UiPass: a view without a camera records no Ui row for World canvases alone" (gpu; with a camera the same UI draws, with its row) failed without the fix (the row's check, 1 of 14).
- **n2. Widget comments did not name the fifth widget** (`UiInteraction.h`, the four widget headers, `UiCanvasComponent.h`'s `UiTheme`, `UiExtraction.h`; correctness). Confirmed. Fixed: the five components exclude each other, each widget's header names `UiTextInput` among those it excludes, and the theme's comments list it. The registered `UiCanvas.Theme` description, decision 37's frozen text, named the four widgets too; the lead amended it after the fix pass ("buttons, toggles, sliders, scroll views and text inputs"), with the regenerated references.
- **n3. The UI preview's comments still said scene pixels where the landing made them the game frame's view pixels** (`UiAuthoring.h`, `UiAuthoringOverlay.h`; correctness). Confirmed. Fixed: `UiPreviewFrame::Scale` is panel pixels per frame pixel, `MapUiPreviewPointToScene` gives frame pixels, which the overlay maps to scene pixels through the game view's output mapping, and the overlay lays the UI out at the image's scene resolution (the function keeps its frozen name).
- **n4. Comment lines past the wrapping width** (`Combined2DUiGoldenTests.cpp`, `QuadBatcher.cpp`, `RenderPassDeclaration.h`, `UiPass.h`; two parentheticals in a row in `RenderPassDeclarationTests.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed. Fixed: re-wrapped, and the parentheticals merged.
- **n5. Includes out of order after the merge** (`AssetTypeRegistration.cpp`, `UiExtractionTests.cpp`, `UiLayoutTests.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed. Fixed: alphabetical within their groups.
- **n6. An unused `TextureData.h` include kept by the merge** (`EditorCore/Automation/AssetMethods.cpp`; correctness). Confirmed: main had dropped it with M21's move of the sprite-sheet texture check. Fixed: removed.
- **n7. `project.upgrade`'s registered description and the game-debugging skill omitted tilemaps** (`ProjectMethods.cpp`, `game-debugging/SKILL.md`; determinism). Confirmed. Fixed: both lists name tilemaps, and so do Architecture §13.5's `project.upgrade` row and §13.12's audit paragraph, which had the same omission; the references and the MCP catalogue regenerated.
- **n8. Architecture §2.3's Lint row and ReviewChecklist §3 named only `RenderSnapshot.h` and `DebugDrawList.h` as NVRHI-free** (determinism). Confirmed. Fixed: both name `UiDrawList.h` too.
- **n9. Stale "the later lander makes it" texts, the frozen-interfaces table, the texture list and the export count** (`UiSystem.h`, `PlaySession.h`, this record; tests). Confirmed. Fixed: the two header comments state the as-built rule (the game view's scene resolution through `ComputeGameViewOutputMapping`); decisions 3, 24 and 50 say where it was done ("Landing" a to c); the frozen table gives `StringTable` 13 and gains a table of the landing's and the milestone review's interfaces (`GetCameraOutputSettings`, `ComputeGameViewOutputMapping`, the ray's PixelPerfectCamera rule, `NonEditingNavigation`, `AppendUiDrawItems`' alpha); "Landing" states that its scaffolds and the review's import no texture, so M22's complete list for M23's `Compression: None` is decision 7's `Rgba.png`; and the landing's export count says what it counts: the export suite's "83 suite(s)" are the configured `Testing.Suites`, of which 77 ran in each exported mode (the six `Modes: ["Editor"]` suites do not run exported), with the 11 replays: 88 entries and 287 cases in `FeatureTest-Release.json` and `-Dist.json`.
- **n10. The vendor cache was documented as eleven libraries** (`build-and-test/vendor-cache.md`, Backlog §6; tests). Confirmed. Fixed: thirteen, HarfBuzz and SheenBidi since M22; Backlog §6's size is about 1.8 GB a set, HarfBuzz's entries measured in the cache adding about 0.3 GB.
- **n11. The golden-images skill's scene list lost a comma** (tests). Confirmed. Fixed.
- **n12. Architecture §1.2 listed only W22.1's static UI as what M22 lifted** (tests). Confirmed. Fixed: it names the interactive UI, the text input, fallback fonts with shaping and bidirectional text, and World canvases, with their decisions.

**Replays.** Every committed replay keeps its hash: `Scaffold.py` re-recorded FeatureTest's byte for byte (only `UiFallback.jsonl`'s new files, `FeatureTest.eproj`'s new suite and `Automation/Provenance.json` changed), and the determinism suite verified all eleven at their committed hashes in the Editor Debug, Editor Release, exported Release and exported Dist runs: `Combined2DUi` `bd51214863ea28c9`, `Determinism` `0770c2168e5843fa`, `Input` `2eb8b6ae8e849235`, `MultiScript` `fd76d22e2d3780ec`, `Physics2D` `a7d3bf019afda673`, `PhysicsSemantics` `157a2e82f75c4dcd`, `Sprites2D` `3a39060058306dc2`, `TextInput` `76aecb0dd6e1153a`, `Tilemap` `c18f9fd5f6ae998e`, `Ui` `14057c3734e9a42b` and `UiWorld` `365eabca1abb20c0`, each with input events failing without it. `TextInput.replay` keeps its hash although its recording suite's Backspace no longer queues a Cancel (m6): the event was queued and delivered within the same tick to no handler, and the state hash holds the queue only across a tick boundary. The World canvases' alpha (M1), the startup transform update (m4), the stale-focus check (m3) and the scene-image rule (m1) change nothing in a lockstep session's ticks, as the hashes show; the demos keep theirs (the games suite).

**Runs** (a checkpoint's targeted runs, strict, ADR 0028 decision 11): `Generate.py`; `Build.py --jobs 12` of Debug, Release (20 projects each) and Dist (17), 0 warnings; the clang-cl portability builds as `CI.py` makes them (`Generate.py --toolset clang` into `bin-int/Portability/windows-vs2026-clang`, then Release's Tests and every Dist project: 0 first-party warnings, only vendored code's tolerated ones); the regression cases, each alone on a Tests build of the code before its fix, failing as each finding above says (ten unit cases, among them the three editor cases on a build with the landing adaptation each covers removed, and one gpu case under Vulkan 1.4), then passing with the fixes; `CheckBuildConfig.py` (102 vendor-cache entries), strict `Lint.py` (0 findings) and `Lint.py --self-test` (37 of 37), `Format.py --check`, `CheckRuntimeMap.py --self-test`, `AuditProvenance.py --self-test`, every fixture generator's `--check`, `GenerateDetMathReference.py --check` and `FetchAssets.py defaults --check` and `fixture --check`, all passed; `GenerateDocs.py --config Debug` (`Automation.md`, `Methods.json` and the MCP catalogue: `project.upgrade`'s description) and its `--check` (22 verified); `Scaffold.py` (FeatureTest reproduced but for the new scaffold's files, its suite and provenance) and `Scaffold.py --check` (byte for byte); the unit suite in full in Debug (4,531 cases passed) and Release (4,530), the 39 child-process targets skipped; the gpu suite in Debug under Vulkan 1.4 and 1.3 (440 cases each, none skipped); the golden suite in Release (34 goldens matched on `nvidia-62x`); feature Debug and Release (95 entries, 84 suites and the 11 replays, 295 cases, the six editor-mode gates green, FeatureTest unchanged); export Release and Dist (84 configured suites, 78 run in each exported mode, and 11 replays: 89 entries and 288 cases each, the six gates green, the coverage merge green); the automation suite in full (790 tests, method coverage 105 of 105); determinism Debug, Release and Dist (the 11 replays above in the four runs, which agree, each with input events failing without it); games Debug, Release and Dist (Tetris, Breakout and NativeTest: the audits, both editor halves with the upgrade dry run clean, the Dist halves, every demo replay at its committed hash and failing without its input), all passed.

## Gate and landing

M22 landed second in phase 2, after M21 (ADR 0028 decision 17's merge train), so its milestone-end gate is `CI.py --require-clang-cl` (ADR 0028 decision 12). After the milestone review's commit `b8f0ea78`, the lead amended `UiCanvas.Theme`'s registered description to name text inputs, which the theme tints too, with the regenerated references (`8d98c5e1`; "Milestone review", n2). One `CI.py --require-clang-cl`, from the slot's root on `8d98c5e1`, passed at the first run in 1 h 29 min (2026-11-10, strict mode): generate, the lint stage, the Debug, Release and Dist builds with the references, the Dist map check and the bake; unit Debug and Release; gpu Debug and Release under API 1.4 and 1.3; golden Release on `nvidia-62x`; feature Debug and Release; automation Release; export Release and Dist; determinism Debug, Release and Dist; games Debug, Release and Dist (Tetris, Breakout and NativeTest at their committed hashes); the portability stage with the clang-cl Release and Dist builds. The hardening stage is phase 2's last lander's (M23's `CI.py --milestone`, ADR 0034 "Gate and landing").

main fast-forwarded to `milestone/m22` (`8d98c5e1`), and is pushed to `origin` at the phase end, after M23's gate (ADR 0028 decision 14).

## Approvals

None for W22.1 or W22.2: nothing is downloaded or vendored. C22.3 vendors HarfBuzz 15.0.0 (Old MIT, with Microsoft's MIT licence of the Universal Shaping Engine data its compiled `hb-ot-shaper-use-table.hh` derives from, `src/ms-use/COPYING`, which stream S restored at W22.3: a second permissive licence file of the same approved upstream archive, recorded by W22.3's review, no new approval) and SheenBidi v2.9.0 (Apache-2.0) and pins the Noto Sans Arabic, Hebrew and CJK JP fixture fonts (SIL OFL 1.1) for stream F, all approved by the product owner on 2026-10-19 (`Docs/Decisions/0001-approvals.md`, ADR 0028 decision 7), each from its official upstream with its SHA-256 and licence (decisions 41 and 42). Nothing else needs the operator. `0001-approvals.md`'s HarfBuzz and SheenBidi row names both HarfBuzz licences (W22.3's review, the lead's authorization) and points to each `VENDOR.md`, and its Noto row to `Scripts/FetchAssets.py`, which stream F fills (the fonts are not built in, so they go to `Tests/Data/Assets/Fonts/`, as the row allows).

## Risks

1. **The draw list is laid out twice.** Extraction lays the UI out for each view and the session for its phases; both are the same pure function, so they agree at one scene resolution, and the hit test (W22.2) reads the session's. A view at another size (a screenshot) shows its own layout, which `ui.tree {width, height}` reproduces.
2. **Text without content sizing.** A label is not sized to its text (decision 6's limit): builders give text rectangles room; `ui.tree` reports each rectangle so an agent sees overflow, and W22.3's measurement layer can feed a later content-size rule without a signature change.
3. **Glyph runs and image runs interleave.** Painter's order splits the pass into a run per change of kind, so a UI that alternates panels and labels records one draw per element; UI element counts are small, and a run never reorders draws.
4. **The scene resolution with M21.** In W22.1 it is the view's size; the later lander of M21 and M22 wires PixelPerfectCamera's into the session's phases and extraction in one place (decision 3), checked by the combined test. Done at M22's landing (`GetCameraOutputSettings`, `ComputeGameViewOutputMapping`; "Landing").
5. **AssetType values.** StringTable is 12 on M22's branch; if M21 lands first, M22 renumbers to 13 at its merge and rebuilds its checkouts' cooked caches (the values live only in cooked headers). M21 landed first: StringTable is 13 ("Landing").
6. **The registered surface ahead of its implementation.** The contract registers components, `ui.tree` and `Loc` so the streams share stable references; until W22.1's integration the coverage gates name them uncovered, which no contract checkpoint runs.
7. **The edit VM's locale** rests on decision 17's amendment, granted on main (2026-10-29); stream L adds the initializer, and until it lands the edit evaluation reads each table's fallback, a difference only `script.eval {context: edit}` sees.
8. **UI determinism (plan risk 8).** Focus and navigation must run only from latched step input: UiInput reads the step view alone, against the current layout, which in every deterministic session is the frame's of the tick (one frame per tick), and the ui block covers every member scripts read; the Ui replay in the four runs is the proof (decision 36).
9. **The surface registered by the streams** (decision 20). Until A, B and C land, the new members of W22.1's components, the Ui settings section, the Ui module and the callbacks exist in the headers and this record only: nothing reads or writes them, the references do not show them, and the wave's integration regenerates the references and FeatureTest once with all of them.
10. **M21's editor files.** The UI preview and its overlay hook into the scene viewport and the editor host, which M21 changes for its 2D editor mode: the calls are single lines into M22's own files, and the later of M21 and M22 adapts them after merging main (ADR 0028's ledger). `viewport.setOptions` may gain an option from each.
11. **The combined 2D/UI test.** A button pressed through PixelPerfectCamera at scale 3 needs the session's pointer mapping and scene resolution to be the camera's; UiInput maps the pointer through the session's output mapping (decision 24), the one place the later lander changes with decision 3's scene resolution. A World canvas's pointer ray is the inverse of the primary camera's projection and pose as render extraction gives them (decision 50), which M21's PixelPerfectCamera replaces (orthographic, pixel-snapped): the later lander routes the ray through it with a case (the review of C22.3). Done at M22's landing, with the combined test ("Landing").
12. **HarfBuzz's size and portability** (plan §8 risk 9). 5.6 MB of sources in one translation unit; MSVC and clang-cl build it (decision 58's runs), but its GCC, Clang and Apple Clang builds are never compiled (ADR 0028 decision 10).
13. **The QuadBatcher amendment** (decision 57). World canvases' images need a depth-tested LDR target in a file M21 owns for a change in phase 2; the lead granted the amendment on main in `1707cf47`, stream W builds the target on its text, and the later of M21 and M22 appends the target after M21's at its merge.
14. **Shaping cost per frame.** `MeasureText` creates its HarfBuzz faces from the cooked bytes per call, and the Ui pass measures every text every frame; UI texts are few, and a cache that keeps the function pure is stream S's to add if a measurement asks for it.
15. **Atlases and goldens.** FontImporter 5 bakes the glyphs the default features reach after the codepoint glyphs, so Inter's atlas changes deliberately and UI text in the default font is shaped (decisions 43 and 44, the lead's decision 2 of the review of C22.3): the Text, Ui and UiWidgets goldens may move, and W22.3's integration regenerates exactly the images that move, each reviewed and explained here (the ledger's reviewed regeneration); the version 5 case pins Inter's new atlas hash, recorded by stream S, and the codepoint glyphs' fields. No replay hash can change through shaping or the atlas (decision 44; its amendment at W22.3's integration: the Ui replay's moved with UiCanvas's registered World-canvas fields). At W22.3's integration no existing golden moved ("Integration (W22.3)").
16. **Text in a World canvas** is drawn untonemapped in the SceneOverlay stage, as world Text is, and depth-tested without depth writes, so two World canvases overlap in their centres' back-to-front order rather than per pixel, and a World canvas draws over world Text where they overlap, whichever is nearer, since world Text is recorded first in the Text row (accepted limits, Backlog §6; the second added by the review of C22.3).

- **The streams and the integration:** decision 19.

- **C22.3:** decision 58's checkpoint runs, then one review of the frozen interfaces (ADR 0028 decision 11); W22.3's streams and integration as decision 58 says.
